# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/mediatek,mt2701-hifsys.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `MediaTek HIFSYS clock and reset controller`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `mediatek,mt2701-hifsys`, `mediatek,mt7622-hifsys`, `mediatek,mt7623-hifsys`. The MediaTek HIFSYS controller provides various clocks and reset outputs to the system.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`mediatek,mt2701-hifsys`, `mediatek,mt7622-hifsys`, `mediatek,mt7623-hifsys`), required properties (`reg`, `#clock-cells`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `reg` max 1 item(s), `#clock-cells` 1, `#reset-cells` 1. clock provider uses `#clock-cells = 1`; reset provider uses `#reset-cells = 1`; MMIO `reg` is max 1 item(s).

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and plain property constraints handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as clock-controller and provide schema smoke coverage.

State and persistence behavior: The binding persists hardware description in DTS and compiled DTB files. Clock, reset, and power-domain identifiers referenced by consumers become stable ABI between board DTS files, dt-binding headers, and Linux providers. The schema stores no runtime state; boot-time state appears when the matched driver maps `reg`, registers providers, and applies any parent clock, assigned-clock, reset, or power-domain relationships encoded in the node.

Dependencies and integration points: Depends on core Devicetree meta-schema only, with driver/header coupling implied by compatible strings. Integration is primarily with MediaTek clock, reset, syscon, infracfg, and topckgen drivers consume these compatibles and publish clock IDs from `dt-bindings/clock/mt*-clk.h` headers. Downstream device nodes consume the exported resources through phandles, so `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`, `#power-domain-cells`, `clock-names`, and header-defined IDs must remain synchronized with provider drivers and DTS users. Key control inputs modeled here are compatible, register range, and provider cell declarations.

Risks: The main risk is ABI drift: changing compatible strings, provider cell counts, required properties, or header IDs can break existing DTS files or driver probing. A wrong `reg` range can bind the driver to the wrong MMIO block, and missing parent clocks, power domains, resets, supplies, or assigned-clock data can defer probe or leave hardware ungated. Because this schema forbids unknown properties, new board-specific properties need explicit schema support.

Test signals: Run Devicetree schema checks such as `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/mediatek,mt2701-hifsys.yaml` and DTS validation for boards using `mediatek,mt2701-hifsys`, `mediatek,mt7622-hifsys`, `mediatek,mt7623-hifsys`. Good signals include clean example validation, no unresolved `$ref` or include paths, provider cell counts matching dt-binding headers, and boot logs showing the matching clock/reset driver registering expected providers without probe deferral.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/mediatek,mt2701-hifsys.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/mediatek,mt7621-sysc.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `MT7621 Clock`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `mediatek,mt7621-sysc`, `syscon`. The MT7621 has a PLL controller from where the cpu clock is provided as well as derived clocks for the bus and the peripherals. It also can gate SoC device clocks. Each clock is assigned an identifier and client nodes use this identifier to specify the clock which they consume. All these identifiers could be found in: [1]: <include/dt-bindings/clock/mt7621-clk.h>. The clocks are provided inside a system controller node. This node is also a reset provider for all the peripherals. Reset related bits are defined in: [2]: <include/dt-bindings/reset/mt7621-reset.h>.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`mediatek,mt7621-sysc`, `syscon`), required properties (`compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`, `ralink,memctl`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `reg` max 1 item(s), `#clock-cells` 1, `#reset-cells` 1, `ralink,memctl` references `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `clock-output-names` max 8 item(s). clock provider uses `#clock-cells = 1`; reset provider uses `#reset-cells = 1`; MMIO `reg` is max 1 item(s).

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and plain property constraints handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as sysc:sysc and provide schema smoke coverage.

Clock, reset, and power-domain identifiers referenced by consumers become stable ABI between board DTS files, dt-binding headers, and Linux providers. The schema stores no runtime state; boot-time state appears when the matched driver maps `reg`, registers providers, and applies any parent clock, assigned-clock, reset, or power-domain relationships encoded in the node.

Dependencies and integration points: Depends on dt-binding headers `dt-bindings/clock/mt7621-clk.h`, `dt-bindings/reset/mt7621-reset.h`, `include/dt-bindings/clock/mt7621-clk.h`, `include/dt-bindings/reset/mt7621-reset.h`; schema refs `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`. Integration is primarily with MediaTek clock, reset, syscon, infracfg, and topckgen drivers consume these compatibles and publish clock IDs from `dt-bindings/clock/mt*-clk.h` headers. Downstream device nodes consume the exported resources through phandles, so `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`, `#power-domain-cells`, `clock-names`, and header-defined IDs must remain synchronized with provider drivers and DTS users. Key control inputs modeled here are compatible, register range, and provider cell declarations.

Risks: The main risk is ABI drift: changing compatible strings, provider cell counts, required properties, or header IDs can break existing DTS files or driver probing. A wrong `reg` range can bind the driver to the wrong MMIO block, and missing parent clocks, power domains, resets, supplies, or assigned-clock data can defer probe or leave hardware ungated. Because this schema forbids unknown properties, new board-specific properties need explicit schema support.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/mediatek,mt8186-fhctl.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `MediaTek frequency hopping and spread spectrum clocking control`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `mediatek,mt6795-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8173-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8186-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8192-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8195-fhctl`. Frequency hopping control (FHCTL) is a piece of hardware that control some PLLs to adopt "hopping" mechanism to adjust their frequency. Spread spectrum clocking (SSC) is another function provided by this hardware.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`mediatek,mt6795-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8173-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8186-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8192-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8195-fhctl`), required properties (`compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `reg` max 1 item(s), `clocks` 1-30 item(s). MMIO `reg` is max 1 item(s).

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and plain property constraints handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as fhctl:fhctl and provide schema smoke coverage.

State and persistence behavior: The binding persists hardware description in DTS and compiled DTB files. Clock, reset, and power-domain identifiers referenced by consumers become stable ABI between board DTS files, dt-binding headers, and Linux providers. The schema stores no runtime state; boot-time state appears when the matched driver maps `reg`, registers providers, and applies any parent clock, assigned-clock, reset, or power-domain relationships encoded in the node.

Dependencies and integration points: Depends on dt-binding headers `dt-bindings/clock/mt8186-clk.h`. Integration is primarily with MediaTek clock, reset, syscon, infracfg, and topckgen drivers consume these compatibles and publish clock IDs from `dt-bindings/clock/mt*-clk.h` headers. Downstream device nodes consume the exported resources through phandles, so `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`, `#power-domain-cells`, `clock-names`, and header-defined IDs must remain synchronized with provider drivers and DTS users. Key control inputs modeled here are parent input clocks.

Risks: The main risk is ABI drift: changing compatible strings, provider cell counts, required properties, or header IDs can break existing DTS files or driver probing. A wrong `reg` range can bind the driver to the wrong MMIO block, and missing parent clocks, power domains, resets, supplies, or assigned-clock data can defer probe or leave hardware ungated. Because this schema forbids unknown properties, new board-specific properties need explicit schema support.

Test signals: Run Devicetree schema checks such as `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/mediatek,mt8186-fhctl.yaml` and DTS validation for boards using `mediatek,mt6795-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8173-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8186-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8192-fhctl`, `mediatek,mt8195-fhctl`. Good signals include clean example validation, no unresolved `$ref` or include paths, provider cell counts matching dt-binding headers, and boot logs showing the matching clock/reset driver registering expected providers without probe deferral.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/mediatek,mt8186-fhctl.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/mediatek,mt8186-sys-clock.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `MediaTek System Clock Controller for MT8186`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `mediatek,mt8186-mcusys`, `mediatek,mt8186-topckgen`, `mediatek,mt8186-infracfg_ao`, `mediatek,mt8186-apmixedsys`, `syscon`. The clock architecture in MediaTek like below PLLs --> dividers --> muxes --> clock gate The apmixedsys provides most of PLLs which generated from SoC 26m. The topckgen provides dividers and muxes which provide the clock source to other IP blocks. The infracfg_ao provides clock gate in peripheral and infrastructure IP blocks. The mcusys provides mux control to select the clock source in AP MCU. The device nodes also provide the system control capacity for configuration.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`mediatek,mt8186-mcusys`, `mediatek,mt8186-topckgen`, `mediatek,mt8186-infracfg_ao`, `mediatek,mt8186-apmixedsys`, `syscon`), required properties (`compatible`, `reg`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `reg` max 1 item(s), `#clock-cells` 1, `#reset-cells` 1. clock provider uses `#clock-cells = 1`; reset provider uses `#reset-cells = 1`; MMIO `reg` is max 1 item(s).

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and plain property constraints handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as topckgen:syscon and provide schema smoke coverage.

State and persistence behavior: The binding persists hardware description in DTS and compiled DTB files. Clock, reset, and power-domain identifiers referenced by consumers become stable ABI between board DTS files, dt-binding headers, and Linux providers. The schema stores no runtime state; boot-time state appears when the matched driver maps `reg`, registers providers, and applies any parent clock, assigned-clock, reset, or power-domain relationships encoded in the node.

Dependencies and integration points: Depends on core Devicetree meta-schema only, with driver/header coupling implied by compatible strings. Integration is primarily with MediaTek clock, reset, syscon, infracfg, and topckgen drivers consume these compatibles and publish clock IDs from `dt-bindings/clock/mt*-clk.h` headers. Downstream device nodes consume the exported resources through phandles, so `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`, `#power-domain-cells`, `clock-names`, and header-defined IDs must remain synchronized with provider drivers and DTS users. Key control inputs modeled here are compatible, register range, and provider cell declarations.

Risks: The main risk is ABI drift: changing compatible strings, provider cell counts, required properties, or header IDs can break existing DTS files or driver probing. A wrong `reg` range can bind the driver to the wrong MMIO block, and missing parent clocks, power domains, resets, supplies, or assigned-clock data can defer probe or leave hardware ungated. Because this schema forbids unknown properties, new board-specific properties need explicit schema support.

Test signals: Run Devicetree schema checks such as `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/mediatek,mt8186-sys-clock.yaml` and DTS validation for boards using `mediatek,mt8186-mcusys`, `mediatek,mt8186-topckgen`, `mediatek,mt8186-infracfg_ao`, `mediatek,mt8186-apmixedsys`, `syscon`. Good signals include clean example validation, no unresolved `$ref` or include paths, provider cell counts matching dt-binding headers, and boot logs showing the matching clock/reset driver registering expected providers without probe deferral.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/nuvoton,npcm750-clk.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `Nuvoton NPCM7XX Clock Controller`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `nuvoton,npcm750-clk`. Nuvoton Poleg BMC NPCM7XX contains an integrated clock controller, which generates and supplies clocks to all modules within the BMC. External clocks: There are six fixed clocks that are generated outside the BMC. All clocks are of a known fixed value that cannot be changed. clk_refclk, clk_mcbypck and clk_sysbypck are inputs to the clock controller. clk_rg1refck, clk_rg2refck and clk_xin are external clocks suppling the network. They are set on the device tree, but not used by the clock module. The network devices use them directly. All available clocks are defined as preprocessor macros in: dt-bindings/clock/nuvoton,npcm7xx-clock.h and can be reused as DT sources.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/nvidia,tegra124-car.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `NVIDIA Tegra Clock and Reset Controller`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `nvidia,tegra124-car`, `nvidia,tegra132-car`. The Clock and Reset (CAR) is the HW module responsible for muxing and gating Tegra's clocks, and setting their rates. It comprises CLKGEN and RSTGEN units. CLKGEN provides the registers to program the PLLs. It controls most of the clock source programming and most of the clock dividers. CLKGEN input signals include the external clock for the reference frequency (12 MHz, 26 MHz) and the external clock for the Real Time Clock (32.768 KHz). Outputs from CLKGEN are inputs clock of the h/w blocks in the Tegra system. RSTGEN provides the registers needed to control resetting of each block in the Tegra system.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`nvidia,tegra124-car`, `nvidia,tegra132-car`), required properties (`compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `reg` max 1 item(s), `#clock-cells` 1, `#reset-cells` 1. clock provider uses `#clock-cells = 1`; reset provider uses `#reset-cells = 1`; MMIO `reg` is max 1 item(s).

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and patternProperties handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as car:clock-controller and provide schema smoke coverage.

State and persistence behavior: The binding persists hardware description in DTS and compiled DTB files. Clock, reset, and power-domain identifiers referenced by consumers become stable ABI between board DTS files, dt-binding headers, and Linux providers. The schema stores no runtime state; boot-time state appears when the matched driver maps `reg`, registers providers, and applies any parent clock, assigned-clock, reset, or power-domain relationships encoded in the node.

Dependencies and integration points: Depends on dt-binding headers `dt-bindings/clock/tegra124-car.h`; schema refs `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`; composition/conditional keys `patternProperties`. Integration is primarily with Tegra CAR/DFLL clock drivers coordinate CPU/SoC clocks, resets, supplies, thermal calibration, and OPP data. Downstream device nodes consume the exported resources through phandles, so `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`, `#power-domain-cells`, `clock-names`, and header-defined IDs must remain synchronized with provider drivers and DTS users. Key control inputs modeled here are compatible, register range, and provider cell declarations.

Risks: The main risk is ABI drift: changing compatible strings, provider cell counts, required properties, or header IDs can break existing DTS files or driver probing. A wrong `reg` range can bind the driver to the wrong MMIO block, and missing parent clocks, power domains, resets, supplies, or assigned-clock data can defer probe or leave hardware ungated. Because this schema forbids unknown properties, new board-specific properties need explicit schema support.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/nvidia,tegra124-dfll.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `NVIDIA Tegra124 (and later) DFLL FCPU clocksource`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `nvidia,tegra124-dfll`, `nvidia,tegra210-dfll`. The DFLL IP block on Tegra is a root clocksource designed for clocking the fast CPU cluster. It consists of a free-running voltage controlled oscillator connected to the CPU voltage rail (VDD_CPU), and a closed loop control module that will automatically adjust the VDD_CPU voltage by communicating with an off-chip PMIC either via an I2C bus or via PWM signals.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`nvidia,tegra124-dfll`, `nvidia,tegra210-dfll`), required properties (`compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `#clock-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `clock-output-names`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `nvidia,sample-rate`, `nvidia,droop-ctrl`, `nvidia,force-mode`, `nvidia,cf`, `nvidia,ci`, `nvidia,cg`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells` 0, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `resets` 1-2 item(s), `vdd-cpu-supply`, `clock-output-names`. clock provider uses `#clock-cells = 0`.

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and allOf handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as clock and provide schema smoke coverage.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/nvidia,tegra20-car.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `NVIDIA Tegra Clock and Reset Controller`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `nvidia,tegra20-car`, `nvidia,tegra30-car`, `nvidia,tegra114-car`, `nvidia,tegra210-car`. The Clock and Reset (CAR) is the HW module responsible for muxing and gating Tegra's clocks, and setting their rates. It comprises CLKGEN and RSTGEN units. CLKGEN provides the registers to program the PLLs. It controls most of the clock source programming and most of the clock dividers. CLKGEN input signals include the external clock for the reference frequency (12 MHz, 26 MHz) and the external clock for the Real Time Clock (32.768 KHz). Outputs from CLKGEN are inputs clock of the h/w blocks in the Tegra system. RSTGEN provides the registers needed to control resetting of each block in the Tegra system.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`nvidia,tegra20-car`, `nvidia,tegra30-car`, `nvidia,tegra114-car`, `nvidia,tegra210-car`), required properties (`compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `reg` max 1 item(s), `#clock-cells` 1, `#reset-cells` 1. clock provider uses `#clock-cells = 1`; reset provider uses `#reset-cells = 1`; MMIO `reg` is max 1 item(s).

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and patternProperties handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as car:clock-controller and provide schema smoke coverage.

State and persistence behavior: The binding persists hardware description in DTS and compiled DTB files. Clock, reset, and power-domain identifiers referenced by consumers become stable ABI between board DTS files, dt-binding headers, and Linux providers. The schema stores no runtime state; boot-time state appears when the matched driver maps `reg`, registers providers, and applies any parent clock, assigned-clock, reset, or power-domain relationships encoded in the node.

Dependencies and integration points: Depends on dt-binding headers `dt-bindings/clock/tegra20-car.h`; composition/conditional keys `patternProperties`. Integration is primarily with Tegra CAR/DFLL clock drivers coordinate CPU/SoC clocks, resets, supplies, thermal calibration, and OPP data. Downstream device nodes consume the exported resources through phandles, so `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`, `#power-domain-cells`, `clock-names`, and header-defined IDs must remain synchronized with provider drivers and DTS users. Key control inputs modeled here are compatible, register range, and provider cell declarations.

Risks: The main risk is ABI drift: changing compatible strings, provider cell counts, required properties, or header IDs can break existing DTS files or driver probing. A wrong `reg` range can bind the driver to the wrong MMIO block, and missing parent clocks, power domains, resets, supplies, or assigned-clock data can defer probe or leave hardware ungated. Because this schema forbids unknown properties, new board-specific properties need explicit schema support.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/nxp,lpc1850-cgu.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `NXP LPC1850 Clock Generation Unit (CGU)`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `nxp,lpc1850-cgu`. The CGU generates multiple independent clocks for the core and the peripheral blocks of the LPC18xx. Each independent clock is called a base clock and itself is one of the inputs to the two Clock Control Units (CCUs) which control the branch clocks to the individual peripherals. The CGU selects the inputs to the clock generators from multiple clock sources, controls the clock generation, and routes the outputs of the clock generators through the clock source bus to the output stages. Each output stage provides an independent clock source and corresponds to one of the base clocks for the LPC18xx. Above text taken from NXP LPC1850 User Manual.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`nxp,lpc1850-cgu`), required properties (`compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `#clock-cells`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible` nxp,lpc1850-cgu, `reg` max 1 item(s), `#clock-cells` 1, `clocks` max 5 item(s), `clock-output-names` 1-28 item(s). clock provider uses `#clock-cells = 1`; MMIO `reg` is max 1 item(s).

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/qcom,ipq9574-cmn-pll.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `Qualcomm CMN PLL Clock Controller on IPQ SoC`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `qcom,ipq5018-cmn-pll`, `qcom,ipq5424-cmn-pll`, `qcom,ipq6018-cmn-pll`, `qcom,ipq8074-cmn-pll`, `qcom,ipq9574-cmn-pll`. The CMN (or common) PLL clock controller expects a reference input clock. This reference clock is from the on-board Wi-Fi. The CMN PLL supplies a number of fixed rate output clocks to the devices providing networking functions and to GCC. These networking hardware include PPE (packet process engine), PCS and the externally connected switch or PHY devices. The CMN PLL block also outputs fixed rate clocks to GCC. The PLL's primary function is to enable fixed rate output clocks for networking hardware functions used with the IPQ SoC.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/qcom,kpss-acc-v1.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `Krait Processor Sub-system (KPSS) Application Clock Controller (ACC) v1`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `qcom,kpss-acc-v1`. The KPSS ACC provides clock, power domain, and reset control to a Krait CPU. There is one ACC register region per CPU within the KPSS remapped region as well as an alias register region that remaps accesses to the ACC associated with the CPU accessing the region. ACC v1 is currently used as a clock-controller for enabling the cpu and handling the aux clocks.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `qcom,nord-nwgcc`, `qcom,nord-segcc`. Qualcomm global clock control (NW, SE) module provides the clocks, resets and power domains on Nord SoC. See also: include/dt-bindings/clock/qcom,nord-nwgcc.h include/dt-bindings/clock/qcom,nord-segcc.h

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`qcom,nord-nwgcc`, `qcom,nord-segcc`), required properties (`compatible`, `clocks`, `#power-domain-cells`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `clocks`. provider cell count is inherited or not the main contract for this binding.

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and allOf handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as clock-controller and provide schema smoke coverage.

State and persistence behavior: The binding persists hardware description in DTS and compiled DTB files. Clock, reset, and power-domain identifiers referenced by consumers become stable ABI between board DTS files, dt-binding headers, and Linux providers. The schema stores no runtime state; boot-time state appears when the matched driver maps `reg`, registers providers, and applies any parent clock, assigned-clock, reset, or power-domain relationships encoded in the node.

Dependencies and integration points: Depends on dt-binding headers `dt-bindings/clock/qcom,nord-nwgcc.h`, `dt-bindings/clock/qcom,nord-segcc.h`, `dt-bindings/clock/qcom,rpmh.h`; schema refs `qcom,gcc.yaml#`; composition/conditional keys `allOf`. Integration is primarily with Qualcomm common clock framework drivers match these `qcom,*` compatibles and expose GCC/MMCC/GPUCC/DISPCC/CAMCC/RPM/RPMh or PLL clock IDs from matching dt-bindings headers. Downstream device nodes consume the exported resources through phandles, so `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`, `#power-domain-cells`, `clock-names`, and header-defined IDs must remain synchronized with provider drivers and DTS users. Key control inputs modeled here are parent input clocks.

Risks: The main risk is ABI drift: changing compatible strings, provider cell counts, required properties, or header IDs can break existing DTS files or driver probing. A wrong `reg` range can bind the driver to the wrong MMIO block, and missing parent clocks, power domains, resets, supplies, or assigned-clock data can defer probe or leave hardware ungated. Because this schema forbids unknown properties, new board-specific properties need explicit schema support.

Test signals: Run Devicetree schema checks such as `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/qcom,nord-nwgcc.yaml` and DTS validation for boards using `qcom,nord-nwgcc`, `qcom,nord-segcc`. Good signals include clean example validation, no unresolved `$ref` or include paths, provider cell counts matching dt-binding headers, and boot logs showing the matching clock/reset driver registering expected providers without probe deferral.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/qcom,nord-nwgcc.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/qcom,rpmcc.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `Qualcomm RPM Clock Controller`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `qcom,rpmcc-apq8060`, `qcom,rpmcc-apq8064`, `qcom,rpmcc-ipq806x`, `qcom,rpmcc-mdm9607`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8226`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8660`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8909`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8916`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8917`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8936`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8937`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8940`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8953`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8974`, and 13 more. The clock enumerators are defined in <dt-bindings/clock/qcom,rpmcc.h> and come in pairs:: FOO_CLK followed by FOO_A_CLK. The latter clock is an "active" clock, which means that the consumer only care that the clock is available when the apps CPU subsystem is active, i.e. not suspended or in deep idle. If it is important that the clock keeps running during system suspend, you need to specify the non-active clock, the one not containing *_A_* in the enumerator name.

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`qcom,rpmcc-apq8060`, `qcom,rpmcc-apq8064`, `qcom,rpmcc-ipq806x`, `qcom,rpmcc-mdm9607`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8226`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8660`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8909`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8916`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8917`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8936`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8937`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8940`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8953`, `qcom,rpmcc-msm8974`, and 13 more), required properties (`compatible`, `#clock-cells`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `#clock-cells` 1, `clocks` 1-2 item(s), `clock-names` 1-2 item(s). clock provider uses `#clock-cells = 1`.

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and allOf handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as the embedded example node(s) and provide schema smoke coverage.

Downstream device nodes consume the exported resources through phandles, so `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`, `#power-domain-cells`, `clock-names`, and header-defined IDs must remain synchronized with provider drivers and DTS users. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/qcom,sa8775p-dispcc.yaml

Purpose: YAML Devicetree schema for `Qualcomm Display Clock & Reset Controller on SA8775P`. It documents and validates nodes with compatibles `qcom,sa8775p-dispcc0`, `qcom,sa8775p-dispcc1`. Qualcomm display clock control module provides the clocks, resets and power domains on SA8775P. See also: include/dt-bindings/clock/qcom,sa8775p-dispcc.h

Important APIs/types/functions: This file is a schema contract rather than runtime code. Its externally visible API is the accepted `compatible` set (`qcom,sa8775p-dispcc0`, `qcom,sa8775p-dispcc1`), required properties (`compatible`, `clocks`, `power-domains`, `#power-domain-cells`), and provider/consumer ABI described by `compatible`, `clocks`, `power-domains` max 1 item(s). provider cell count is inherited or not the main contract for this binding.

Control flow: There is no executable control flow. Validation flows through `dt-schema`: the YAML document is loaded against the core meta-schema, a DTS node's `compatible` selects this binding, `required` enforces mandatory fields, and allOf handle variant-specific shape. The examples exercise nodes such as clock-controller and provide schema smoke coverage.

State and persistence behavior: The binding persists hardware description in DTS and compiled DTB files. Clock, reset, and power-domain identifiers referenced by consumers become stable ABI between board DTS files, dt-binding headers, and Linux providers. The schema stores no runtime state; boot-time state appears when the matched driver maps `reg`, registers providers, and applies any parent clock, assigned-clock, reset, or power-domain relationships encoded in the node.

Dependencies and integration points: Depends on dt-binding headers `dt-bindings/clock/qcom,rpmh.h`, `dt-bindings/clock/qcom,sa8775p-dispcc.h`, `dt-bindings/clock/qcom,sa8775p-gcc.h`, `dt-bindings/power/qcom-rpmpd.h`; schema refs `qcom,gcc.yaml#`; composition/conditional keys `allOf`. Integration is primarily with Qualcomm common clock framework drivers match these `qcom,*` compatibles and expose GCC/MMCC/GPUCC/DISPCC/CAMCC/RPM/RPMh or PLL clock IDs from matching dt-bindings headers. Downstream device nodes consume the exported resources through phandles, so `#clock-cells`, `#reset-cells`, `#power-domain-cells`, `clock-names`, and header-defined IDs must remain synchronized with provider drivers and DTS users. Key control inputs modeled here are parent input clocks, power-domain attachment.

Risks: The main risk is ABI drift: changing compatible strings, provider cell counts, required properties, or header IDs can break existing DTS files or driver probing. A wrong `reg` range can bind the driver to the wrong MMIO block, and missing parent clocks, power domains, resets, supplies, or assigned-clock data can defer probe or leave hardware ungated. Because this schema forbids unknown properties, new board-specific properties need explicit schema support.

Test signals: Run Devicetree schema checks such as `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/qcom,sa8775p-dispcc.yaml` and DTS validation for boards using `qcom,sa8775p-dispcc0`, `qcom,sa8775p-dispcc1`. Good signals include clean example validation, no unresolved `$ref` or include paths, provider cell counts matching dt-binding headers, and boot logs showing the matching clock/reset driver registering expected providers without probe deferral.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/clock/qcom,sa8775p-dispcc.yaml -->
